## Spellmoot

A Spellmoot (or spellmoot) was an annual meeting of mages related to spells. It was considered a smaller form of the Magefairs once held every decade.Since the Magefair has changed from being held every decade to every year, it appears that it supplanted the annual Spellmoot.

History
The custom of spellmoots was an ancient one. The sage Alphraxis, an author who wrote on the history and nature of Skullport, recorded that the Netherese wizards who founded the Sargauth Enclave in the 8 century before Dalereckoning chose their Underdark location so they could still attend annual spellmoots on the surface.

Halaster Blackcloak, before his madness, was credited with beginning the tradition of holding Spellmoots annually and Magefairs every ten years and doing so in different, remote locations around Faerûn.

## Spendelarde

Spendelarde was a wizard and spell developer.

Magic
He was noted as the inventor of several utilitarian spells, including the necromancy spell Spendelarde's chaser.

Possessions
He once owned the magical staff Spendelarde's Protector, which he carried with him when traveling.

History
Spendelarde studied magic under Mentor Wintercloak, one of the Seven Wizards of Myth Drannor.It is unclear when Spendelarde's studies under Mentor occurred. It could have been as early as the founding of the Seven Wizards in 379 DR (Cormanthyr: Empire of the Elves, p. 39). Although Mentor Wintercloak left the Seven Wizards in 682 DR (The Fall of Myth Drannor, p. 18), he taught several students later in life, and Spendelarde could be among these.

Activities
Although Spendelarde did not travel often, he occasionally went on long and dangerous treks to distant locales.

## Spotted plague

The spotted plague, sometimes called spotted fever, was one of the Three Plagues of Faerûn, a disease highlighted by blistering boils that burst, leaving scattering spots of pock marks. It was regarded as one of the most dangerous non-magical diseases, even more than smallpox or typhus.

Acquisition
The spotted plague was spread by skin-to-skin contact and took effect in one day.

Effects
When infected, an individual's skin looked flush and their flesh broke out in swollen, white pustules referred to as "spots" or "festers". They eventually burst and left behind permanent pocks and scars.

The infection ran throughout the internal structures of the body, rotting away nearly all types of tissues. It caused a temporary loss of balance, reasoning, and sense of spatial awareness, and occasionally vision. In nearly all cases the sense of smell was lost, and this was nearly always permanent.

The spotted plague usually killed within 24 hours of the first symptoms. If a victim of the disease survived past the first two days and nights, a time fraught with mortality, they were usually ensured survival as the plague ran its course. These fortunate souls were often extremely sick for a matter of days and experienced notable weakness for around a month.

History
In Hammer of the, an outbreak of spotted plague struck Iriaebor, but was contained with only 20 casualties thanks to the quick collective efforts of the churches of Chauntea, Eldath, and Selûne. Given the strangeness of a plague striking in winter, the outbreak was blamed on followers of Talona, specifically a group of tattooed individuals who had been seen in town a few tendays prior.

## Spider leap

Spider leap was a spell used by drow. It was very rarely used by other races.

Effects
This spell was a specialized version of dimension door. A caster used this spell to teleport themselves to a location within 30 feet (9 meters) in which a spider was seen or has touched. If several spiders are within this area, the spell randomly selects one and transport the caster there. The spell was able to pass through all physical obstructions except for water or magical barriers. The spell prevented the caster from transporting to a location too small for the caster. If the targeted spider was on a wall and the caster arrived in mid-air, the spell did not prevent the caster from falling. The spell did not influence a spider nor protected the caster. The spell was able to transport not only the caster but also their equipment, up to a limit of 500 pounds (227 kilograms).

Components
This spell required only a verbal component.

## Spell thrust

Spell thrust was an arcane abjuration spell that stripped all magical protections that were imbued by spells as powerful as minor spell turning, or those less powerful.

Effects
Spell thrust could only be cast upon a single target, from up to 25 feet (7.6 meters) away. That target instantly had their minor to moderate spell protections stripped, such as those granted from minor spell turning, spell immunity, and several others of the same power or lower.

Creatures with any degree of magic resistance would find that it did not help against this spell.

Spider spawn was a conjuration spell that could summon one or two spiders. The summoned arachnids would comply with the commands of the spellcaster via a telepathic link.

If uninterrupted, the eggs would magically transform into a spider, or two spiders (but this only had a one in five chance). The type of spider summoned was dependent on the power of the caster, with novice mages only being able to summon giant spiders, but more experienced casters were able to summon phase spiders, or even sword spiders.

Spirit guardians was a spell used by clerics.

When cast, this spell summoned several spirits who protected the caster. They remained within  of the caster. The spirits matched the alignment of the caster. ## Spider shapes

Spider shapes was a divine transmutation spell that transformed its subjects into spiders. It was unique to clerics who worshiped deities with the Spider domain.

Effect
This spell functioned much in the same manner as spells like polymorph other. The caster could transform several willing creatures into monstrous spiders of any size, though he or she could not change different creatures into different-sized spiders with the same casting. Up to seventeen creatures, or more for more experienced casters could be affect, provided they were within 30 feet of each other in close and began 65 feet (20 meters) or more from the caster. The spell had no effect on unwilling creatures.

Those transformed stayed in spider form for the duration of the spell (17 hours or more) or until the caster dismissed it for the whole group. Individuals could elect to return to their normal forms, but this ended the spell for that individual.

Body parts removed from the spiders did not revert to their original forms. A side-effect of this was these spiders had venomous bites.

Unlike with polymorph other, those transformed with spider shapes suffered no disorientation. Furthermore, they also became refreshed and healed as if they'd received a full day's rest.

Components
It required verbal and somatic components, as well as a divine focus.

## Spell focus

A spell focus or focus was a basic tool used in spellcasting.

A focus was simply an item of some kind that was necessary for successful casting of certain spells. Unlike material components, spell foci were not consumed by the casting of the spell and could be reused. Like material components, spell foci were usually small enough to store in a spell component pouch and were very cheap, but there were notable exceptions to this.

## Spellgaunt

Spellgaunts were magical spiders capable of feeding on arcane energy. Despite their magical abilities, they were only animals, although as all spiders, they were associated with Lolth.

Description
They were large spiders, with multifaceted eyes and golden skin covered in hair. Its legs were twice as long as a fully grown human. It could distend its mouth in a scissors-like fashion, and it was filled with long fangs that dripped with golden saliva. Its abdomen had spinnerets capable of producing large quantities of golden silk. When wounded, it bled a light blue ichor.

Abilities
Spellgaunts were capable predators. They were drawn by arcane energy, which they fed on, and could bite and eat magical items. Said items could be irreparably damaged in a few bites or stripped of their magic.

Despite its size, it was a very agile creature, capable of leaping on its prey and even contorting mid-leap in order to avoid dangerous weapons or threats. It was also capable of shooting large streams and swaths of golden silk from its spinnerets in order to entangle its opponents.

Despite its rare abilities, it wasn't very intelligent, as it would try to feed on magical energy that might be too dangerous, a decoy, or a trap.

History
Cavatina Xarann faced one of these creatures on her own. She dispelled its invisibility and fought with it, realizing its magic-consuming nature when she noticed that her sword had been dented and had bite marks, which wouldn't be possible to do for a mundane spider. Cavatina ended up conjuring a swarm of magical blades—the spellgaunt tried eating them for their arcane energy, but ended up cut to shreds. However, her sword Demonbane was completely voided of its magic.

Spell fangs was a spell that was first developed by the phaerimms.

## Spider staff

A spider staff was an ornate adamantine quarterstaff.

Description
Spider staves appeared to be as long as drow were tall, and were black in color. Close to the top of the staves were small black-and-silver protrusions that potentially functioned as grips where they were held. The uppermost parts of spider staves were fashioned into the shape of spiders.

Powers
In addition to bludgeoning opponents as would standard quarterstaves, spider staves poisoned their foes when hit. The staves would also allow the wielder to cast spider climb and web without the need for spellcasting components, but they could only access this power if they had potential to learn the spells individually. As it is an adamantine weapon, each successful hit against an object hit would be a critical hit.

Each spider staff could hold up to ten charges, and expended one each time spider climb was cast, and two for each time web was. They could recharge six to ten charges a day, but had a chance of being destroyed in the process.

Notable Owners
 Nezznar

## Spectral eyes

Spectral eyes was an uncommon spell used by necromancers.

Effects
A caster used this spell to see through the eyes of either a skeleton or a zombie. The creature had to be within  of the caster for the spell to function properly. This distance increased as the caster's skill level increased. Additionally, the spell allowed the caster to command the creature, providing limited movement. If the targeted creature was already under the command of another caster, this aspect of the spell failed. If the creature wandered out of the area of effect, the visual link was severed and the spell ended.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, the spell required a material component, a preserved eye.

History
This spell was known to be used by wizards of the Cult of the Dragon.

## Spellcaster (spell)

Spellcaster was a spell used by casters to store spells.

Effects
With this spell, a caster was able to store a spell in an object. Once stored, the newly enchanted object was able to repeatedly cast the spell. The object had to be stationary and had to have a value of more than 10,000 gp. A caster first cast this spell on the desired object and then cast the spell they wished stored. Any low level spell, except dispel magic, could be stored. If for some reason the enchantment was lifted from the object, the object was not damaged.

Casting
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component: a hinge made from platinum. The hinge had to have a minimum value of 500 gp.

History
This spell was known to be used by wizards of the Cult of the Dragon.

## Spell shield

Spell shield was a spell sometimes used by abjurers, clerics and incantatrixes. Otherwise it was rarely used.

Effects
When cast, the spell bestowed a strong defense against some magical attacks, effects, and spells. The caster either cast the spell on themselves or on another individual. To cast the spell onto another individual, the caster had to physically touch the targeted individual.

History
The specifications of this spell could be displayed by the relic known as the Scepter of Mystra.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, the spell required a material component, a diamond. The diamond could be of any grade or size. It was consumed by the spell during casting. The divine version of this spell required only verbal and somatic components.

Protection from magic energy was a spell used by wizards.

When cast, any spells used against the caster were absorbed. The spell was only effective if a spell was cast upon the caster directly. The spell was also effective against spell-like abilities.

It possessed the lion-like body, humanoid head, spiked tail, and leathery wings of a corporeal manticore. This creature was unfailingly loyal to the casting cleric, was immune to charming effects, and was completely silent.

The spectral stag could be dispelled and destroyed by antimagic fields.

The targeted creature had to be within  of the caster while the spell was cast. This range increased as the caster's skill level grew.

## Speechlink

Speechlink was a spell used by bards.

Effects
When cast, this spell allowed the caster and a targeted creature to communicate instantly, even over great distances. The targeted creature had to be willing for the spell to function. The spell transmitted only the voice of the individual, it did not transmit any background noise. Either individual could cancel the spell at any time. While the spell could be cast on any creature, even animals, the spell did not provide translation or comprehension for different languages.

Spetums were roughly similar in appearance to ranseurs, having a pair of sharp tapering points Though their shafts were generally shorter, at around  in length. They also sometimes had their side blades angled backwards.

Weaponology
Like all spears, the primary function of spetums was to be thrusted at and pierce their foes. Their design's secondary capability was deflecting attacks, as well as catching and holding foes at a distance when penetration wasn't achieved.

The wielder of such a weapon could strike more quickly than usual, and as such, it allowed them the time to cause an additional hit. However, if already under the effect of a spell that increased the amount of times they could strike, such as haste, the wielder would not have an additional attack on top of that.

Spell reflection caused all spells reaching the target to be returned to their caster. If the spell did damage, the caster would suffer it; otherwise, the spell was negated.

The Spell-Lord's House was a temple to Azuth in the city of Calimport.

Location
The Spell-Lord's House was located in Fikra Drudach of Hapij Sabban within Quill Ward.

Structure

The temple was a beautiful six-story structure. Its most notable feature was its eastern wall, which contained a massive,  rose window, with panes composed not of glass but of permanent illusory colors. These colors constantly swirled about.

Interior
The Spell-Lord's House contained one of the oldest monasteries to the High One in FaerûnFor reference, the Sixth Age of Calimshan ended circa, and the mortal Azuth obtained apotheosis circa . and one of the best libraries in the city that was not privately owned.

The temple was first constructed at the end of the Sixth Age of Calimshan but had been rebuilt at least four additional times.

## Spell mirror

Spell mirror was a wizard spell from the school of alteration.

Effects
This spell was cast on a reflective object such as a mirror. The mirror was then placed in a strategic location as a trap. Any medium-powered spell, or discharge from a magic item, cast at the mirror would harmlessly enter the mirror and charge it. The mirror held the charged spell until it was either broken or looked at by targets specified by the caster, at which point the stored spell was released at the targets.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components a mirror and a handful of dust from an object that was once enchanted to cast. The mirror was damaged during the casting.

## Spellstorm (spell)

Spellstorm was a wizard spell from the school of alteration.

Usage
This spell created a sphere centered on the caster that moved around with them. It caused every weapon within a  diameter to spring forward and attack a target of the caster's choosing. The caster did not need to know the locations of each weapon, nor could any physical barriers, such as chests, keep the weapons from becoming involved in the spell. There was no limit to the number of weapons that would be affected by this spell as long as they were within range.

Additionally, no mundane weapons, including those launched from outside of the spell's affected area, could harm the caster while the spell remained active.

Components
This spell only required verbal and somatic components to cast.

Sphere of wonder was a wizard spell from the schools of alteration and evocation.

However, at the time of casting the caster could choose to allow certain types of spell or magic items to work properly. The caster could select fire-based spells to function for example.

## Spotted lion

Spotted lions, also called cave lions, were a species of large, plains-dwelling cat closely related to the lion.

Description
Spotted lions resembled regular lions, but were said to be 30% to twice again as large. Their distinctive dappled fur was dusky, as was the mane, with charcoal-grey spots.

Ecology
This species lived on warm, open plains, but was rare outside of "lost world" regions. Otherwise, their diet and social habits were akin to those of the common lion. Including hunting in prides; they were known to take prey as large giant elk or even mammoths.

Both stone and cloud giants were known to keep spotted lions as pets.

Spotted lions were among the felids that might be summoned by the spell pride of Nobanion.

History
Fracto-Nimbuli, a cloud giant in Chondath, kept three pet spotted lions.

Speed was a type of giant rune.

Effects
When a creature wore footwear inscribed with this rune, they were capable of moving at far greater speeds. If this rune was extensively studied a second time, then this effect would be even greater.

Components
Like most giant runes, it required no material components to cast, though the caster needed to know the giant rune healing. The process of shaping this rune generally took around ten to forty minutes and it needed to be inscribed upon a set of any kind footwear. Even horseshoes were known to work for this purpose.

## Spark

Spark was a personal cantrip that beginning wizards could learn. It created a big blue bolt of electricity at a location chosen by the caster.

Effects
The caster could choose any point within  for the spark to appear.The original description of this cantrip in Dragon #100 gave an increasing range based on the level of the caster, and high-level casters could create multiple sparks in quick succession at the target location. Creatures targeted by spark had a chance to resist the magic of the spell and, if successful, received only an uncomfortable shock, otherwise, they suffered minimal damage. Metals and other conductive materials could transmit the energy released, therefore, a person wearing metal armor was slightly more likely to be shocked. However, if the target creature was very near death, this cantrip could not finish them off.This caveat was added in the Wizard's Spell Compendium Volume Four. Atmospheric conditions, such as dry air, humidity, or a nearby storm, had no effect on the size or puissance of the spark.

Components
This cantrip required verbal and somatic components to cast. The caster simultaneously snapped their fingers and made a popping sound.

History
This cantrip was invented by Sabirine, a mage who supported the use and improvement of cantrips. Spark was recorded in the tome known as Sabirine's Specular, along with catfeet and snatch.

Notes

Spice was a useful cantrip that initiate wizards could learn. It conjured a sprinkling of a particular spice or herb above a target creature or object.

This cantrip conjured a small quantity of a spice known to the caster. It appeared just above the target and fell as a fine powder or small flakes. The amount was typically enough to treat food or drink for about six people.The original description of this cantrip in Dragon #59 put this at a dozen people. It was changed to "half-dozen" in Wizard's Spell Compendium Volume Four. The target of this spell had to be within  of the caster.

This cantrip required verbal and somatic components to cast. The caster hummed a little tune while pretending to crumble and sprinkle a substance with one hand.

## Spider (cantrip)

Spider was a personal cantrip that acolyte wizards could learn. It summoned a common, normal-sized spider to a nearby location chosen by the caster.

Effects
This cantrip conjured a single common spider. It appeared at a spot within The original description of this spell in Dragon #60 used the 1-edition nomenclature 1", meaning  indoors and  outdoors. This was simplified to the shorter range in Wizard's Spell Compendium Volume Four. of the caster and arrived in an irritated state. If summoned upon a living creature, the spider immediately tried to bite, distracting the target for a few seconds. There was a slight chance that the conjured spider was venomous, in which case the target took minimal damage and had a chance to resist, or else fall ill for a few days. The effects of spider venom could be reduced with slow poison or removed with neutralize poison.

## Spice

Spice, also known as deg in Dwarvish, was a broad term for a variety of seeds, roots, and bark that were primarily used for seasoning or flavoring food. They were often dried, ground up into a powder, and mixed together with a variety of other spices. Over time the advancement of long distance trade caused spices that were once exclusive to a single region to be spread far and wide across Toril.

Travelers on Toril typically kept spices in little glass vials, encased in whittled wooden 'shielding shells' to guard against breakage. These were carried inside of drawstring leather pouches, known as spice pouches. Such pouches had the "finished" side as their interior, thus minimizing reactions with the vials' contents.

Varieties
Spices used throughout Toril included the following:
 Allspice
 Almond
 Arispeg
 Ateris, a spice that was endemic to the Thunder Peaks and Storm Horns.
 Awana, a spice that was endemic to the island of the same name.
 Bentilan, a spice that was endemic to the the Thunder Peaks and Storm Horns.
 Berbere, a spice mixture unique to the people of Chult.
 Canella
 Cardamon
 Chilis
 Cinnamon
 Clove
 Cocoa
 Coffee
 Cubeb
 Curry
 Delph
 Fagara, a spice that was endemic to the country of T'u Lung.
 Fire lichen, a spice that was endemic to the Underdark.
 Funguswood, common mushroom used as a wood substitute and sometimes as a savory spice.
 Galingale
 Garlic
 Ginger
 Lingale, a broth thickener.
 Mace
 Marka
 Maxoris, a spice used as a preservative native to the lower Tun River.
 Mingari, a spice that was endemic to Ulgarth.
 Myrrh
 Nutmeg
 Panjar, a spice that was endemic to Tymanther.
 Paprika
 Pepper
 Peral, a choking vine found around the Storm Horns and Thunder Peaks.
 Priadoran fire spice, a spice that was endemic to the region of Priador.
 Rosemary
 Saffron
 Sesame
 Tamarind
 Tanbark, a spice that was endemic to Anauroch.
 Tep yen, a spice sold in Almorel.
 Thyme
 Turmeric
 Vanilla, a spice that was endemic to Maztica.
 Yellow safre, a spice known to the yaqubi of the Khopet-Dag.
 Zedoary

History
In 920 DR, the merchant Windyn Balindre returned to Faerûn from Kara-tur with some of that land's unique spices in tow.

In 1238 DR, a major trade war began between the Amnian merchant families House Ulvax and House Ophal. This was due to the latter causing House Ulvax's spice trade with Zakhara to dwindle. The feud between them would persist for over a hundred years, igniting numerous other trade wars. Eventually, it would culminate in the Murann Spice War in 1338 DR. The Council of Six would go on to punish House Ulvax for instigating the conflict by revoking their right to engage in the Zakharan spice trade.

At some point prior to 1352 DR, General Hie Aie Shek cleared out bandits that were scouring the Spice Road.

From 1356 DR  to 1374 DR the Highmoon Trading Coster was known to trade the spices arispeg, marka, and delph.

Around 1359 DR, the following spices were known to be carried by caravans along the Spice Road: Camphor, Caramon, Ginseng, Myrrh, Nutmeg, and Saffron.

Around 1367 DR, a trio of traveling merchants that hailed from Anauroch were trading a variety of spices in Shadowdale in return for silver. Some of their spices included curry, pepper, and rock salt.

For many decades the four rival trading families of Marsember shipped spices to and from lands all across the Inner Sea. Over time they became the city's main export and earned it the moniker "City of Spices." In the years leading up to 1370 DR the city's dominance in the spice trade waned as other kingdoms started getting involved in exporting condiments to the Sword Coast, such as Westgate and Nimpeth.

Around 1370 DR, Mithral Hall's alliances with surface kingdoms allowed for the importation of many spices that were once unknown to denizens of the Underdark. Also during this time, spices were one of the few industries of trade that the nation of Thay had with the lands west of it.

Around 1372 DR, rare spices were known to travel across the Black Road. Around this time an investment of 100 gp worth of western goods could fetch over 500 gp worth of silk and spices from Kara-Tur.

During the Time of Troubles, House Se'Sehen were known to trade rare spices in return for either slaves or a means by which to transport their spies to northern ports.

In the late 15 century, merchants from further south were known to trade spices in the city of Bryn Shander. Around this same time, merchants were known to travel to Port Nyanzaru from as far away as the Sword Coast in order to purchase the unique spices of Chult.

Prices
In the bazaars of Zakhara the native spices could generally be bought for around 7 silver pieces to 2 gold pieces per pound.

In the 14 century DR, markets in Waterdeep sold most spices for around 5 copper pieces to 8 gold pieces per dry, though saffron in particular sold for 40 gold pieces per dry .

Notable Users of Spices
General
 Throughout Faerûn meat was typically coated in a mixture of powdered spices, such as sage and rosemary, before being spit-roasted. Calishites were particularly known for heavily spicing their meat.
 A small number of people carried their own blend of spices when traveling.A modern example given of spice blends was garam masala.
Groups
 Spices were among the raw material trade goods that the Rundeen dealt in.
 The Shad'iar, the nomadic tribes of the Land of the Lions, often used spices on their simple foods. Whenever a clan, family, or tribe of Shad'iar hosted members of another tribe they would slaughter and cook animals over a great roaring fire. To enhance the flavor of that meat, they would either rubs spices into it or throw them over the bonfire. Spices were also commonly used by them as a barter good.

Races & Intelligent Creatures
 Elves considered cooking a delicate art form and their taste buds generally could not handle heavy use of spices. However, among elves the more adventurous youth were known to partake in over-spiced food.
 Cloud giants were known to prefer their food carefully prepared with a variety of spices. Furthermore, those who worshiped the deity Stronmaus would ritually start each morning by scattering handfuls of spice and incense into the wind.
 The ghul-kin of Zakhara were known to have a fondness for highly spiced meat and stews.
 Gnomish cuisine was rarely known to employ spices.
 Halflings did not favor a lot of spices in their food, with the exception of onions. They generally preferred a mixture of herbs over strong spices when it came to seasoning their traditional sausages, cheeses, stews, and baked goods.
 Rakshasa were typically known to season their food with all manner of exotic spices.
Regions & Settlements
 The Calimshan city of Suldolphor sported an entrance known as the Spice Gate. Also within that city, the Shaldizar Inn would throw spices on to fires in order to scent the interior air.
 In the nation of Tethyr, spices were one of many goods that people commonly bartered with.
 Most travelers in the Western Heartlands carried varied arrays of the spices that they considered "base staples." These included cinnamon, dill seeds, ground black pepper, ground garlic, nutmeg, or thyme.
 In the lands further south and east of the Western Heartlands the spices considered "base staples" to travelers were ground almonds, ground ginger, dried tamarind, raisins, rosemary, and turmeric.

Trivia
 Among the many valuable items that made up the treasure hoards of some dragons and rocs, spices could be found.
 Sailors of phlogiston rarely had good access to spices.
 The dwarven deity Vergadain often demonstrated his favor with individuals through unexpected discoveries, most notably of rare spices.
 In Zakhara, burning expensive spices could summon a Sakina.

Appendix
Notes

See Also
 Herbs, another broad term for a variety of plant-life that is often used to season food.
 Spice, a cantrip for creating spices.
External Links

References

The Spirit of the Forest was an entity that became trapped in Icewind Dale.

The Spirit of the Forest appeared much larger than a humanoid. It had huge, stag-like horns and a human-like body.

As of the 14 century DR, the Everspring Tree became corrupted by the crystal cultists' foul magic. In its corrupt state, it trapped the Spirit of the Forest in its strong roots.

## Sperm whale

Sperm whales, also known as cachalots, were large carnivorous whales.
Abilities
Sperm whales were powerful swimmers. They could swim up to  in a single day, and dive to a depth of, although it has been reported they could dive as far down as .
Behavior
Sperm whales lived in groups called pods. They communicated with each other via whale-song. They maintained positive relations with good-aligned sentient aquatic species. Sperm whales were curious about surface-dwelling humanoids but cautious around those who hunted whales.
Combat
Sperm whales defended themselves by using their massive tail as a weapon. Sperm whales were large enough to swallow entire boats in one gulp. Sperm whales were hunted as prey by other large sea creatures such as the Zakharan kraken.
Habitat
Sperm whales lived in oceans and seas. They were known to inhabit the waters of the Sea of Fallen Stars.
Biology
Sperm whales were carnivorous and hunted large fish and octopuses. Their primary prey were giant squids.
Uses
Sperm whales naturally produced spermaceti. This waxy substance was used by wizards to craft white candles that were used in spellcasting. Additionally, sperm whales produced ambergris, which was very rare and could sell for up to 20,000 gp. Due to their habit of swallowing entire boats whole, treasure could be found within the stomach of a sperm whale. The teeth of sperm whales were used in scrimshaw.
Notable Sperm Whales
 Angajuk, a talking sperm whale that had the ability to speak Common.

Magic items and large items were not affected by this cantrip.

## Spell Breaker

Spell Breaker was an enchanted long sword that greatly aided those who fought against arcane spellcasters.

Description
The sword featured a silver hilt decorated with translucent blue gemstones.

Powers
Spell Breaker possessed a +2 enchantment and had a chance to dispel any magical effects on any person or creature it struck.

Additionally it offered its wielder significant protections against spells and spell-like effects. This effect was amplified for those martial warriors and paladins who hunted down mages.

History
By the, the sword was lost somewhere in the Western Heartlands, not far from the Boareskyr Bridge.

## Sprout

Sprout was a useful cantrip that neophyte wizards could learn. It caused live seeds or plants to experience an immediate growth spurt, or caused vegetable matter to ripen or age.

Effects
The target of this spell had to be within  of the caster and affected plant matter in a volume no bigger than . This cantrip accelerated the growth of plants. Seeds sprouted to seedlings, seedlings grew about, flower buds bloomed, and so on. If used on fruit or vegetables, they aged, advancing from unripe toward ripe, or possibly overripe to spoiled.

Components
This cantrip required verbal and somatic components to cast. The caster pantomimed hoeing while making a whispery rustling sound.

## Sponge

Sponges were immobile aquatic creatures that lived on sea floors. Their harvest and sale as household objects was lucrative.

Ecology
Sponges lived as far deep as the upper depths, or between .

Uses

When harvested and crushed repeatedly for a time, the skeleton of a sponge became exceptionally absorbent, able to retain a large amount of moisture that could be wrung out later. In this form, sponges were generally available in most towns and markets, including Aurora's Emporium, which acquired them from farms in the Shining Sea off Tharsult. Sponges cultivated in the Sea of Swords were commonly found in use across the Western Heartlands and the city of Baldur's Gate.

The kingdom of Es'daan farmed sponges in great numbers, and the creatures were a staple food source in As'arem and Myth Nantar.

Reef giants were adept foragers of sponges, and were able to bring up whole hoards of them for trade.

Sponges were components for the spells Abi-Dalzim's horrid wilting, numbing sphere, wall of water, and wildshield. A dried sponge was also used in the creation of a potion of growth.

## Spinesheath

The spinesheath was a unique enchanted stiletto.

The blade of this stiletto was specially designed to pierce through armor.

Powers
The spinesheath possessed a +1 enchantment.

History
This stiletto first belonged to an assassin in Calimport by the name of Beledor, until one day he was poisoned by a bribed servant. The weapon's name was derived from his tendency to plunge its blade it into the backs of his targets.

Over time spinesheath changed hands, until ending up in the possession of a lizard king lairing in the Dragon's Eye in 1281 DR.

## Spitting felldrake

Spitting felldrakes, sometimes called spitting drakes, were a species of felldrake best known for their ability to spit acid.

Description
Spitting felldrakes resembled very small, wingless dragons with long, sinuous bodies.

Behavior
Spitting drakes had very low levels of intelligence. However, they were fierce and loyal, and naturally good at heart, thanks to their divine heritage. However, they were also impulsive and eager to attack.

Abilities
Spitting felldrakes were named for their ability to spit acidic venom at targets up to  away. Like other felldrakes, they were immune to magic that caused sleep or paralysis.

Combat
Spitting drakes instinctively attacked one target at a time, singlemindedly attacking until that enemy went down. In groups, they naturally all ganged up on a single enemy.

Ecology
Felldrakes were carnivorous creatures that preferred to live in temperate plains, hills, or forests. Their snaky bodies also made them well-suited for underground expeditions that elves were averse to. On their own in the wild, spitting felldrakes congregated in clutches of 3-5 individuals, and they were occasionally known to live alongside crested felldrakes or in groups dominated by a single rage drake.

Though a bit difficult to manage, some humanoids besides elves were known to have domesticated spitting felldrakes and trained them to act as guards, including humans, kobolds, and goblinoids.

History
Spitting felldrakes were originally created by Bahamut to help guard elves against future invasions after a group of elven wizards helped the Platinum Dragon turn back a demonic incursion.

Around 1479 DR, spitting drakes were domesticated and used by a tribe of goblinoids living within Undermountain.

Around that same time, a spitting felldrake was sucked into Undermountain through one of Halaster Blackcloak's remaining portals. The drake wandered around the dungeon for some time, until it was captured by the vampire Dayan for use in his necromantic rituals.

## Spirit of Wa

The Spirit of Wa was the spirit said to have created the islands of Wa in Kara-Tur in the legends of the Wanese people. It was one of the Eight Million Gods.

History
When the island of Tsukishima was established by the Spirit of Wa, two sisters named the Bloom Lady and the Rock Lady competed for the hand of Ninigi, the August Grandchild.

The Spirit of Wa once banished the "devils" who used to wander the mainland to the Isle of Devils, located off the coast near Aru, where each was trapped unless a human voluntarily agreed to take its place.

The Spirit of Wa was the one who gave the fabled Moonlight Arrow and the Sacred Wand to Kochi, Master of the Peach Tree, making him the first emperor of Wa.

## Speculum Grounds

The Speculum Grounds was the area of Myth Drannor in the City Heights that surrounded the Hall of Wizards, the center of arcane knowledge in the city.

Geography
In front of the main entrance to the Hall of Wizards stood a monument to Mythanthar the archmage of Cormanthyr. The statue had an enchantment cast on it that allowed instantaneous transportation between the Halls of Light in Dwarven Dungeons and Speculum, using the ring of calling and the seal of Mythanthar.

The Hall of Wizards itself resembled a slumbering dragon that curled around its egg. The main entrance to the center of knowledge was shaped like a dragon's head. The huge egg-like structure was a complicated magical device known as the Orrery. The machine was a gently-rotating glowing orbs model of the Realmspace, that was used to efficiently teleport the wizards of the old Myth Drannor between the city's Spinning Spires.

To the east of the Speculum and the monument laid the entrance to the park that was known as the House of Omberdawn, later - Sullymarsh. A winding pathway up to the Windrider Glade was located to the north of Sullymarsh's entrance.

The Gate of Antarn stood to the east from the Speculum and led to the Altar of the Mythal. The gate was magically locked unless they had a specific enchanted glyph tattooed on their bodies. Very few were able to create the tattoo, Annorrweyn Eversong was one of them.

To the west of the Hall of Wizards was located a ruined tower, and further west - the Circle of Ualair the Silent, a once beautifully crafted amphitheater. The amphitheater also served as a hidden entrance to the Elven Catacombs, a huge underground structure that served Castle Cormanthor as a dungeon, prison, and burial catacombs.

The long staircase at the eastern edge of the Speculum Grounds led directly to the path connecting the grounds to the Nightingale Court.

A patch of healing blueglow moss could be found at the Speculum Grounds' southern edge.

History
During the Weeping War, the Speculum Grounds suffered minimum damage, as the defending forces abandoned the area for better defensible positions. Because of that, and robust magical defenses, the Speculum Grounds avoided a big part of battling and destruction. Although over the centuries that followed, the grounds were damaged by time and elements.

The wondrous Orrery was dismantled by the dwarves of the House of Gems during the war and seized functioning with its floating orbs being scattered around the city, enchased in metal. The device was briefly restored to functionality by a group of adventurers from Phlan that opposed the Cult of the Dragon's occupation of Myth Drannor in 1369 DR.

Inhabitants
In 1369 DR, the Speculum Grounds was a dangerous area of the city, filled with preying gargoyles, roaming orcs, vicious arraccats, and vengeful undead. The agents of the Cult of the Dragon is this area were scarce dragon-kin troops.

A ghost of Caalenfaire, a prominent elven diviner haunted the Hall of Wizards, in his eternal compulsive search for knowledge, accompanied by his loyal follower, the floating skull named Volun.

## Spirit of the Sun

The Spirit of the Sun was a spirit of Wa associated with the sun. He was one of the Eight Million Gods.

History
In Wa Year 1663 (1245 DR), the Spirit of the Sun gave Matasuuri Shogoro, the shogun of Wa, three treasures in recognition of his work to unite Wa. These were a sword, a mirror, and a pearl, all glowing with the sun's light.

However, when Matasuuri Nagahide became shogun in Wa Year 1754 (1336 DR), he got the idea that the gifts were unlucky, and gave them away: the sword to the Shining Temple of Bishamon, the mirror to the businessman Oshichiro Yoto of Iiso, and the pearl to Yorifusa Ti, daimyo of Hotomori. Then, in Wa Year 1775 (1357 DR), the Spirit of the Sun visited Nagahide in his dreams, learned he no longer had the treasures, and was offended. The shogun's tairo hired adventurers to retrieve the treasures.

## Spell phylactery

Spell phylactery was a transmutation spell used by clerics. This spell originated from Mystra the goddess of magic.

Effects
This spell created a set of triggering conditions for a scroll so the spell written on the scroll would be instantly cast upon the caster when the conditions were met. For this to work the spell on the scroll needed to be a spell that was memorized by the caster.

The scroll used for spell phylactery needed to be bound to the caster's arm or forehead in some manner. Only scrolls with effects that directly affected the caster could be used.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required the scroll in question to cast.

References

## Spellmantle

Spellmantle was an abjuration spell used by clerics. This spell originated from Mystra.

Effects
This spell surrounded the caster in a protective aura that protected the caster from a specific spell. Casters with more skill could select more than one specific spell for the spellmantle to protect against.

In addition to protecting the caster, the spellmantle allowed the caster to use the absorbed spell energy and redirect it toward healing or to instantly trigger a pre-selected spell upon the caster. If the selected spell was one the caster had currently memorized it did not remove that spell from the caster's memory.

Components
This spell required only verbal and somatic components to cast.

## Spectral wizard

Spectral wizards were a rare form of incorporeal undead created from living spellcasters by use of the create spectral wizard spell.

Description
Spectral wizards were only made from humans, elves, and gnomes.

Combat
As ethereal creatures, spectral wizards could only be harmed by enchanted or silver weapons. Their touch could paralyze their opponent. Their undead nature made them completely immune to any spell involving sleep, hold, fear, cold, charm, polymorph, paralyze, or death magic effects. They could be turned as common spectres.

Spectral wizards were created from once living spellcasters. As such they had access to the same spells they used as living beings.

With extreme effort spectral wizards were able to solidify themselves enough to manipulate physicial objects.

Ecology
A spectral wizard's impact on their surroundings was dictated by the wizard who created them with the create spectral wizard'' spell. All spectral wizards were magically compelled to follow the commands of their creator.

Appendix
References

## Spell shape

Spell shape was a wild magic alteration spell used exclusively by wild mages.

Effects
This spell allowed a wild mage to snatch spell energy, or effects from magic items such as wands, directed at them and reshape it as they desired. Once the caster absorbed the incoming spell energy they could either let it dissipate harmlessly, or instantly direct it back to their attacker with a spell casting. The caster could choose to direct the spell energy to the attacker in the form of any spell they currently had memorized provided the spell was not more powerful than the spell originally directed at them. This act would not remove that spell from the caster's memory. The spell energy could not be saved in any manner. It would dissipate if not used immediately.

A wild mage struck with two or more spells at the same time could choose which spell they wanted to shape, allowing the remaining incoming spells to strike them as normal.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a diamond worth at least 2,000 gp to cast. Casting the spell turned the diamond into a lump of coal.

## Spelltouch

Spelltouch"Dragon" magazine #200 has this spell listed as a 2-level spell and "Wizard's Spell Compendium Volume Three" has it listed as a 4-level spell.  was an enchantment spell used by wizards.

Effects
When cast upon an inanimate object, spelltouch allowed the item to store a beginner to intermediate divine or arcane spell until the item touched a target creature. The caster set the specific type of trigger creature during the casting. The caster could also specify a type of creature that was immune to the stored spell's effects, granting a degree of protection in the event of an explosion. Additionally, the caster did not need to be the one to cast the follow-up spell that was stored in the item.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a pinch of powdered gemstone of any type,  a pinch of ash left by a fire created by an augmented spell, and a drop of water to cast.

## Speculum

The Speculum, also known as the Hall of Wizards, was a great hall used by the wizards and archwizards as a repository or arcane knowledge of the ancient Myth Drannor.

Location

The Hall of Wizards was located on the aptly named Speculum Grounds in the ward of Sheshyrinnam, across the Street of the Evening Star from the Castle Cormanthor with an elven jewelry store to the east and Nightingale Court to the north-west.

Structure
Surrounded by flagstones, the three-story Speculum was easily spotted from afar for it's golden colored dome that resembled a curled up dragon with its head hanging decoratively on top of the main entrance. The Gate of Antarn led to the Altar of the Mythal on the top of the curled up dragon dome. The gate was magically locked unless they had a specific enchanted glyph tattooed on their bodies. Very few were able to create the tattoo, Anorrweyn Evensong was one of them. The altar held the gem of the weave, the heart of Myth Drannor's mythal.

Interior
The main chamber, just past the Speculum gates, was a grand hall of oval shape. The walls were decorated with exquisite murals and mosaics that depicted great wizards and dragons, all intertwined in stylistic images of magic. The depictions of dragons were laid out with gems of correspondence colors for chromatic dragon kin and metals for metallic dragons. The eyes of the decorative dragons were small round windows. The hallways were reinforced with beautifully carved columns and beams, all depicting dragon tails of all colors. The enchanted sources of lights were orbs held by decorative silver dragon claws. The chamber's ceiling was decorated with a single grand mural of a red dragon and a gold dragon locked in aerial combat. Numerous arcane runes were weaved into the decorative patterns all over the walls. Statues of great and accomplished wizards, frozen in mid-spellcasting, stood along the hall's walls.

The grand call had the second level past two rounded dual stairs that went up to a ring of balconies that overlooked the mosaicked first floor. At the base of the stairs, between the two, stood an enchanted pedestal that, once touched, sounded delicate chimes and materialized a magical mirror that could scry for the pedestal users. The second floor's balconies were filled with bookshelves that held countless books, arcane tomes, and scrolls.

Beneath the hall proper were numerous underground levels, the full scope of which were not entirely known.

It was believed there was a secret, extradimensional stronghold kept by Myth Drannan wizards, the entrance of which could be found inside of the Speculum. The arcane chamber was a safe hold for their most powerful magical secrets, originally created by archmage Alaghar of the Horns, protected for years by a deepspawn, even following the city's fall into ruin.

History

Kythorn 25, 714 DR was the date of one of the last battles of the Weeping War. The Battle of Stars Shining took place around the Speculum as a swarming army of yugoloths surrounded the building. A quarter of the invading army was wiped off by a brave scribe halfling Prannith Springheels as he grabbed the staff of the magi that belonged to his dead master and plummeted into the army off the top of Speculum. As he was falling he broke the staff causing a deadly explosion. Spell-Major Josidiah Starym used his Artblade and its magics to collapse part of the building on the army, and incinerate the remaining yugoloths and himself, securing the Speculum (or what was left of it).

When the Cult of the Dragon took over the ruins of Myth Drannor, in the, they set some of their best wizards to the task of breaking into the Speculum and gaining access to the wizards' extradimensional chamber, to no avail.

Inhabitants

During the glory years of Myth Drannor, the Speculum served as the meeting hall for the city's Guild of Wizards, Conjurers, & Enchanters.

In 1369 DR, the Speculum was briefly under the Cult of the Dragon's control. After the cult gave up on unlocking the vaults, they abandoned the Speculum. Shortly after a few orcs of the local tribe took over the building.

Through the years one of Myth Drannor's baelnorns Caalenfaire operated from one of the vaults of Speculum, accompanied by his floating skull aide Volun.

The tribe made their home in the infamous fortress of Big Bone Deep.

Possessions
The Spleen Eaters were the keepers of the Gruumshar relic known as the Eye of Gruumsh.

According to the legends that surrounded the tribe, they had lived in the Stonelands since before the Storm Horn mountains had formed.

In the latter half of the, tribesmen of the Spleen Eaters made a great trek across central Faerûn to recover the Eye of Gruumsh when it was stolen from their home. They were guided by the divine intervention of Gruumsh himself and by his followers from across the region.

## Spitting spider

Spitting spiders were a type of monstrous spider that could be found in the Realms.
Description
Despite being very large, these spiders had surprisingly small bodies.

Abilities
these spiders were capable of spraying a pale yellow poison from their fangs. They were also noted for their agility.

Combat
After spraying the nearest target with poison a spitting spider would pounce upon and whack them with its front legs to further weaken the victim before its toxin took affect. When being used as a mount, they coordinated attacks with their riders.

In the late 15 century DR, spitting spiders could be found in the Gnarlroot Caves beneath Sharandar. Around that time they could also be found in the pit fights conducted by the Gray Wolf tribe in Vellosk.

In the, an expedition of drow slavers from House Xorlarrin laid siege to a small farming town in Rothé Valley and employed many spiders in this effort, including spitting spiders.

By 1493 DR, spitting spiders could be found in many parts of Undermountain.

These creatures could be found in the Underdark of Toril. They were particularly known to be found in the Whispering Caverns.

## Splugoth

Splugoth the Returned, previously known as Splug, was a goblin who briefly collaborated with Acquisitions Incorporated before being betrayed and left for dead by the company. Sometime before the, he was resurrected by the lich Jeff Magic and ended up in charge of the Six's operation to get its hands on the Orrery of the Wanderer.

Description
Splugoth had one pale eye.

Personality
Splug was a pathological liar.

Abilities
Splugoth had a limited ability to stop physical effects using residual energy from Jeff Magic's resurrection magic. He also recalled some words he heard on his way back to the world of the living, which he could recite to momentarily overwhelm those who heard them.

Possessions

By the time he attempted to conduct the ritual to trigger an incursion from the Far Realm, Splugoth might've had any number of components of the Orrery of the Wanderer, at least including the far gear.

Relationships
Due to his betrayal by the company, Splugoth was deeply resentful towards Acquisitions Incorporated. He also had a history with the "C" Team.

Splugoth looked up to Jeff Magic, and considered him to be a "real hero".

History
Years before his first run-in with Acquisitions Incorporated, Splug was part of a goblin tribe that lived beneath Shadowfell Keep, dominated by the sorcerer and death cult leader Kalarel. Unpopular with his fellow goblins, he ended up incarcerated after conning several tribe members out of their ale.

When Acquisitions Incorporated came to the keep in order to stop Kalarel's plan to free what Splug described as a "death god",The identity of this deity is, perhaps intentionally, left ambiguous in the Acquisitions Incorporated sourcebook. In the first season of the Acquisitions Incorporated podcast and the original text of Keep on the Shadowfell, this "death god" was Orcus, but Dungeon magazine 155's Keep on the Shadowfell: Forgotten Realms Conversion instead places Shar in this role. the goblin somehow assisted them in doing so. Despite his help, the company apparently left Splug to die in order to save one of its team members.

Eventually, Splug was resurrected by Jeff Magic, at which point he took on the name Splugoth the Returned. The lich put him in charge of obtaining all components of the Orrery of the Wanderer in the name of the Six, mainly through coordinating raids. In this capacity he hired a number of operatives and mercenaries, including the jackalwere Hamebi, a battle balloon captain named Garla, and her crew of hobgoblins, goblins, and smoke mephits.

Having learned of a group of Acquisitions Incorporated employees interfering with his ambitions, Splugoth arranged with Garla to intercept the group's battle balloon journey to the Greypeak Mountains with two of the Six's own battle balloons, one of them armed with a giant needle affixed to its prow.

Rumors & Legends
It was rumored that Splugoth was resurrected more than once.

## Spiderwalker

Spiderwalkers were a type of construct created by practitioners of hishna from the bodies of dried tarantulas.

Behavior
Spiderwalkers could be given very specific instructions, but only by those who knew their command word. They could be directed to strike at a certain individual, or to fight as many of a certain type as they could encounter.

Spiderwalkers could only be activated at night time and when the first rays of the sun arose they would cease to function, even if the sun was concealed by clouds or the spiderwalker was indoors or underground.

## Spirac

Spirac was the 71 layer of the Abyss. It was an uncivilized place with no ruler.

Description
Spirac was a wild land of deep forests of spiky ferns and thin, towering mountains. It was impossible to build anything on the layer, and all attempts crumbled into weed-covered ruins and then natural rock within months of their creation.

Notable Locations
A mountain pool located near the presumed geographical center of the layer was said to rejuvenate any mortal who bathed in it by 10 to 40 years.

Inhabitants
Spirac was home to a staggering variety of of unusual animals and magical beasts. Many demon lords used Spirac as their personal hunting grounds, and would take offense when they ran into any "intruders"; despite this, hunters flocked to the layer to hunt yeth hounds, vorrs, nightmare beasts, and much worse. Cannier hunters would bring lesser demons such as abyssal skulkers and jariliths to aid them.

Additional, many mortals came to the layer, drawn by the legends of the rejuvenating pool that lay at its center, although most ended up as prey in the bags of hunting fiends. However, those who reached the pool, supposedly, had to sacrifice a portion of their free will to the demon lord Soneillon, called the Youthful Crone, who lived within its depths.

History
Long ago, as the obyriths fell and the tanar'ri rose in power, an alliance of seven demon lords hunted the obyrith lord called the Malgoth on the fields of Spirac. In much more recent times, the demon lord Mastiphal won Malcanthet's favor by presenting her with the head of a huge fiendish smilodon that he'd killed in Spirac.

## Space spider

Space spiders, also known as crystal spiders, were an immensely rare species of spider-like creatures of unknown origin that were a combination of organic and mineral life.

Description
The spider-like bodies of these creatures looked to be glass, more mineral than organic, with multi-colored internal organs. On average an adult space spider's body was around  in diameter.

Behavior
These creatures possessed an exceptional level of intelligence.

Abilities
The pincers and fangs of these creatures were very powerful. If given enough time, they could even chew through a spelljammer's metal hull. Their fangs were laced with poison. However, their bodies could be hurt by the spell shatter.

In addition to the aforementioned abilities, the mothers of space spider nests were capable of jumping over  both horizontally and vertically. This was typically utilized in ambushing intruders or pursuing intruders that had evaded the normal spiders among their colony. And the oldest of diamond spinners possessed a form of telepathy.

Ecology
The mothers within space spider nests were known as diamond spinners. The oldest and wisest among them was known as the master spinner, who guided the construction of their nests through mental commands. These nests were bubble-like mazes.

The crystalline eggs of a space spider were known to always contain diamonds, each worth around ten to a hundred gold pieces each. Whether these were produced naturally by diamond spinners or required a supply of diamonds was unknown. The young within an egg would feed off the diamonds until they fully matured within twenty-one days.

Diet
These creatures were a lithovorous species, meaning they fed upon minerals.

Habitats
Space spiders typically inhabited asteroids that were laden with diamonds.

Languages
How space spiders communicated was a mystery, though they occasionally produced high-pitched tinkling sounds.

Relationships
Vulkaran, the emperor of the Vodoni Empire, had an nest of these creatures guarding the entrance to his palace and this was largely unknown to people outside of his palace.

Rumors & Legends
There were a number of theories regarding the origins of space spiders. Some believed they were the result of an ancient magical experiment, while others thought they originated from the Elemental Plane of Earth. But most scholars proposed that they were distant relatives of xorn, xaren, and most likely crysmals. Some went even further with their similarities to the crysmals, proposing that they were actually a more advanced space-based form of them.

## Spyder-fiend

Spyder-fiends were a thankfully rare subtype of tanar'ri demon that served the demon lord Miska and, on occasion, Cyric the Mad God.

Description
All of the spyder-fiends looked like some variation of a giant spider with a wolf-like head. The smallest spyder-fiends, the kakkuu, were about the size of a pony, being  long and weighing about, while the largest spyder-fiends, the raklupis, were  long and weighed about . Some of the spyder-fiends possessed arms in addition to their eight legs, including the phisarazu, lycosidilith, and the raklupis; spithriku possessed pedipalps. They were typically covered in coarse, dark or sandy brown hair with splotches of other colors, while the fur on the head was grey.

The fur of phisarazu was largely black, some with a blue or green tint, and swirled, striped, and/or spotted with different shades of blue, green, or grey. Lycosidiliths, by contrast, were largely hairless, instead being covered in green or blue chitin with speckles, spots, and/or rings of black, orange, and yellow. Raklupis also lacked fur on their spider bodies, but had a triple row of sharp spines on their back and black or grey fur on the head; the chitin and spines could be almost any color and were always vividly marked with swirls, bands, and spots of contrasting colors.

Abilities

The spyder-fiends had several abilities in common, including infravision, a venomous bite, and the ability to produce silk, with which they could do several things, including binding opponents and catching themselves while falling. They possessed two spell-like abilities, darkness 15' radius and teleport without error. They were completely immune to damage caused by poison, electricity, and nonmagical fire, and were resistant to that cause by magical fire, cold, and poisonous gas.

With the exception of the kakkuu, the spyder-fiends could communicate with telepathy, and the phisarazu, lycosidilith, and raklupis could speak most languages.

Personality
Spyder-fiends were tanar'ri who combined the worst aspects of spiders and wolves. The less intelligent had all the charm of rabid, bloodthirsty wolves, while the more intelligent were manipulative and even eloquent. All were cunning and cruel.

Society
The spyder-fiends were the servants of Miska the Wolf Spider, the second Prince of Demons, and by extension they served his lover, the Queen of Chaos. In fact, some sages speculated that the original spyder-fiends were the offspring of those two. They continued to serve the Queen of Chaos even after their master was locked away.

Spyder-fiends were almost always found only on the 14 layer of the Abyss, the Steaming Fen. If found outside of that hellish place, it was on an errand for their mistress.

History
Circa 1362 DR, several spyder-fiends were under command of Cyric who tasked the creatures to nightly torment Kaverin Ebonhand as part of the payment for his resurrection. The sulfurous demons visited the leader of the Cult of Frost as he fell asleep and whispered horrors of torture eternal into his ears giving him restless sleep filled with nightmares. The creatures glade accepted "gifts" of dead bodies, dragging them to Cyric's realm for some nefarious reason. After Kaverin's second death at the hands of Artus Cimber, Cyric's spyder-fiends arrived to he Prime Material Plane for the last time, to claim Ebonhand's body and should, dragging him towards his eternal torture.

In 1371 DR, spyder-fiends were among the army of fiends that flooded the streets of Eldrinpar of Doegan in attempt to seize one of the Utter Easts bloodforges, shortly after the death of Aetheric III, the emperor of Doegan at the hands of Artemis Entreri.

## Spirit of the air

Spirits of the air were a type of spirit that solely existed to serve the deities that commanded the wind and air, most of whom were elemental in nature.

Description
These spirits resembled monkeys, but stood  tall. They had long, prehensile tails. Their fingers were elongated, forming the ribs of their wings, which some likened to the wings of a bat. They also had clawed feet and sported two tusks that curled up towards their nose, then out towards their cheeks. Their tusked mouths always had a merry expression.

Spirits of the air were born into the world with a white coloration. During the day, as their bodies soaked in the rays of the sun they became very colorful, sort of blond. But during the night, under the light of the moon, their colors would wash away and leave them with a coloration that was either black or dark brown.

They typically dressed in brilliant, shining fabric that was ever-shifting in hue and greater than most silk or spider thread.

Personality
These spirits had an average level of intelligence. They were fierce in their desire to serve and obey their masters, incapable of refusing orders,but were not by nature aggressive. When not serving deities they lived carefree, reveling in the sheer joy of flight.

Abilities
Spirits of air were capable of seeing in areas of low-light and total darkness. And they had a highly developed sense of smell.

They radiated a  radius magical aura in which they could control winds at will. They could do so while performing nearly any activity, with the exception of combat. Finally, a spirit of air could only be harmed by weapons with a +1 or greater enchantment and were immune to air-based effects as well as strong winds.

Once per day a spirit of the air could transform itself into a whirlwind of air.
Magic
Spirits of air possessed an array of spell-like abilities. At will they could cast call lightning and detect magic. Once per day they could cast confusion, control weather, whirlwind, and wind walk.

In addition to their innate abilities, there were a number of divine spells of the Air and Luck domains that spirits of the air could cast. These included the following:
aid, animal messenger, command, comprehend languages, create water, cure minor wounds, cure moderate wounds, dispel magic, divine favor, enthrall, entropic shield, freedom of movement, invisibility purge, light, magic vestment, protection from elements, resistance, summon monster I, summon monster IV, virtue, and wind wall.

Death
When a spirit of the air died their body released all the colors that it had built up in life as an array of arcing rainbows. Bright, merry and playful imp-like beings would also be released by their rainbows, whose existence would last for no more than an hour or two. These creatures could not be commanded or trapped by any means. They lacked knowledge of the world and spoke in a rapid, high-pitched gibbering manner.

Combat
These creatures fought with their clawed feet, teeth, and wings. They also fought with wide range of weaponry that typically included darts, longbows, two-handed swords, and footman's maces, and +1 heavy maces. They sometimes held these weapons with their tails.

With the exception of the last one, the weapons they fought with were not enchanted, but rather imbued with the might of the deity they served. This allowed them to injure any creature that could only be harmed by +3 or greater enchanted weapons. When other creatures tried to wield their weapons they would function mundanely.

When engaged in combat a spirit of the air almost never allowed its feet to touch the ground. They always fought with their spell-like abilities before moving in for close quarters combat. They were also skilled at fighting against airborne enemies.

History
In 1357 DR, some spirits of the air were being commanded by the evil sorcerer Opawang on behalf of their master in the Celestial Bureaucracy. During that time they resided within the mountains of Akari Island.

Society
These creatures lived their whole lives in the air, never touching the ground.
Diet
Homelands
Spirits of the air could be found throughout the Upper Planes, the neutral-aligned planes, and the Elemental Plane of Air. They claimed windy areas as their territories and were generally seen in the upper part of atmospheres, near windy mountaintops, or near the windswept plains of lowlands. Due to their remarkable mobility, the territory for spirits of air could sometimes stretch for hundreds of miles

In Prime Material plane, these creatures were only commonly encountered in the land of Kara-Tur.

Languages
Spirits of the air were capable of speaking Auran, the Draconic language, and the Giant language.
Relationships
Spirits of the air served deities that commanded the air and wind regardless of their alignment. They were particularly favored servants of the goddess Aerdrie Faenya. In some cases they even served non-deific powers that dealt in those same areas, such as the good archomental Chan, who had them keep an eye on the daily issues of her realm.

Any god who could create and control the wind could create and control one of these creatures. The deities that these spirits served were capable of summoning up to a hundred of them each day. They served as either messengers of full-on proxies.

Rumors & Legends
According to an ancient legend, when the gods first made the multiverse, they looked upon their work knowing that it was complete, yet incomplete. The only thing left for it was for it to be destroyed, the final inevitability that would mark it as "complete". Believing themselves to be omniscient, they regarded this fact with happiness and sadness, but their hubris proved the undoing of their work's "finished" state. Their speech had given life to the air around them, and before it could be stopped the words took shape, coalescing into small, silvery entities that mocked their parents' pride.

Angered at their disrespect, the gods drew back in some of the life they had accidentally given, binding the spirits to their will. So it was that the spirits of the air, despite their generally free existence and adherence to no one deity, would forever hear the rhythmic breathing of the wind gods, forced to carry out the secret instructions given to them in a divine sigh lest the breath of creation that made them be re-inhaled.

## Spectral blade

Spectral blade was an enchanted ghostly freezing cold longsword.

Description
The hilt was crafted out of the bones of a creature that had died a painful and violent death. When inactive, a spectral blade appeared to be nothing but the bone hilt. But once the hilt was touched by a creature, a blade made entirely out of cold light, similar in appearance to faerie fire, came to life.

Spectral blades could be created to resemble other types of weapons such as daggers or greatswords.

Powers
The blade of this longsword was intangible and was hard to wield because of its weightlessness. When a target was struck by a spectral blade, it suffered magical damage, similar to the chill touch spell.

History
In 1369 DR, a spectral blade was in the possession of Marjon, a red half-dragon member of the Cult of the Dragon. He was among the members of the cult who was occupying ruined Myth Drannor in attempts to tap into the power of the pool of radiance.

Creation
Spectral blades could be created by a skilled magic arms and armor crafters via use of the chill touch spell.

## Spirreth Dalambra

Spirreth Dalambra was a drow mage retainer of House Freth in the Troglodyte Warrens in Undermountain in the early 1490s DR.

History
Spirreth was the second-in-command of the Freth troops in the Troglodyte Warrens, under his master Beranica Freth. They battled the House Auvryndar army. However, in 1492 DR, Beranica was killed by some demons and Spirreth assumed total command. He was supported by two summoned chasme in the middle of a ferocious battle with Auvryndar troops. If nobody intervened, Spirreth lost the day and escaped alone.

Personality
Spirreth had the task of reporting the death of Beranica to her older sister Erelal Freth; in a desperate ploy, he hoped to send to her some strangers and accuse them of being Beranica's killers.

Spellshields were a type of plant native to the Savage Frontier region. Their berries by themselves were considered to be a herb.

Usage
Digesting the berries of this plant raw imparted a slightly improved chance at resisting the effects of some spells.

## Spear Rock

Spear Rock was a famous shrine to Tempus, Lord of Battles, standing in the western Storm Horns mountains of Cormyr.

Location
It stood on a knoll in the foothills of the mountains, several fields up the slope above the hamlet of Wyvernhunt, and not far from the so-called Wyvernhunt Keep. It could be clearly sighted from the hamlet, and a strong archer here could land an arrow in its market area.

Structure
The shrine was rough, comprising a standing stone that was Spear Rock itself and beside a flat stone called the Shield Stone, which served as an altar table.

Activities
Despite its crude nature, Spear Rock was regularly visited both by lone warriors and priests of Tempus all through the year. The latter cleansed the place and tidied up or collected old offering.

Defenses
By its position and powers, it was an excellent platform for archers.

Legends
The faithful believed Tempus listened attentively to prayers made here. Sometimes, if he liked the prayer, he even answered back from the Shield Stone, giving the location of an enemy named in the prayer. Typically, this was as laconic and wide-ranging as "Suzail" or "Sembia", but on some occasions as precise "north of the Hill of Lost Souls". This had happened several times in the mid-1300s, according to reliable reports.

The church of Tempus attested that several of the faithful who'd prayed at Spear Rock prior to a battle saw their foes smote by bolts of lightning from out of clear skies.

Moreover, a local legend held that an archer faithful to Tempus who stood at the shrine could not miss with bow or crossbow. Elminster observed this was a slight exaggeration, clarifying any missile weapon was substantially more accurate when used within  of the Shield Stone. In any case, it was a fine place for a desperate archer to make a heroic last stand.

History
Such stands happened at least twice in its history before 1367 DR. In one, an outlaw known as Saeragus the Sly was trapped at Spear Rock by a Purple Dragons patrol and managed to take down every one of them with his bow and arrows.

## Spirit essence

Spirit essence was a potent healing potion encountered in north Faerûn, Icewind Dale, and the Spine of the World.

Powers
Spirit essence concoctions were considered valuable by many. They were created in the far north of Faerûn by capturing and distilling spirits of trolls. The process of creation of these potions was so complex that only the most experienced and powerful necromancers and enchanters dared to attempt it. If brewed correctly, this potion healed even the most severe wounds, instantly regenerating bones and tissues.

## Space drake

Space drakes were a very rare variety of drake found in wildspace.

Description
Space drakes were said to resemble wingless radiant dragons, which essentially meant they were legless as well as wingless, with glittering, pearlescent scales. They were smaller than radiant dragons at only about  in length. Space drakes also had a dorsal fin that extended along their head and neck, unlike their larger kin.

Abilities
Despite their lack of wings, space drakes flew through wildspace by way of an innate spelljamming ability. They also had a few innate spell-like abilities, being able to "cast" restore air, detect portal, and light each twice per day.

Space drakes also possessed a breath weapon of pulses of force, said to be similar to the spell magic missile. They could emit either one large pulse or up to six smaller pulses at a time; different smaller pulses could strike different targets. The breathe weapon was effective against both creatures, and large objects such as spelljamming ships' hulls.

Combat
In combat, space drakes attacked with both their jaws and their tails. They were known to wrap around and constrict smaller creatures as well. Space drakes were so flexible that they could bring both their jaws and tail to bear against the same target quite easily.

Ecology
Space drakes lived solely in space, typically wildspace, as they apparently did not like living in the phlogiston. They were territorial, claiming vast regions of wildspace around a lair (such as a hollowed-out asteroid or an abandoned dwarven citadel). Space drakes were normally solitary, although family groups that lived together were not unknown. Juvenile space drakes were called space newts, and resembled their parents except for being smaller and blind. Space newts took only five years to reach adulthood, and space drakes could live up to 1,000 years. While blind, space newts would follow anything that fed them regularly, and once their eyes opened, would imprint on the first creature to feed them.

Space drakes ate anything: plant material, rock, metal, even space dust, although they had a distinct preference for meat.

History
Divad, a human explorer from Toril, was killed while exploring a space drake's lair.

## Spotted Man

The Spotted Man was a retired treasure hunter living in the Mistshore neighborhood of Waterdeep in the late-15 century DR.The date for all Year 1 RPGA sanctioned adventures is considered to be 1479 DR for the purposes of this wiki.

Description
This man's body was riddled in bloated blue spots that burst periodically, weeping a blue tinted blood.

History
The man who would take on the alias "Spotted Man" was quite a successful treasure hunter until one of his adventures left him spellscarred. Just before he contracted the condition he had discovered the location of the wrecked ship Ardent Purveyor and was planning to launch an expedition to loot it. Following his spellscarring, the Spotted Man feared that taking to the open sea or diving underwater would worsen his condition.

In 1479 DR,The date for all Year 1 RPGA sanctioned adventures is considered to be 1479 DR for the purposes of this wiki. a group of adventurers approached him that were in search of the Ardent Purveyor. After some convincing he informed the adventurers that the currents of Deepwater Harbor had placed the wreck not in Umberlee’s Cache, but close to it, and provided a map with some reckoning points.

Spirit mask was a divine alteration spell used only by shamans devoted to Ubtao called spiritlords.

Effects
This spell allowed the spiritlord to entwine their spirit essence with that of a major or minor spirit. The spell diverted divination spells targeted on the caster to the entwined spirit for the spell's duration, allowing the caster to misdirect any such intrusion for information.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components the spiritlord's holy symbol to cast.

## Speak with birds

Speak with birds was a divine alteration spell used only by specialty priests devoted to Thoth called Scriveners.

Effects
With this spell a Scrivener could understand and communicate with any normal or giant bird possessing animal intelligence. The cooperation of the bird was not guaranteed, but the priest was able to ask questions and receive answers. The quality of answers provided by the bird depended on their general nature and intelligence.

Components
This spell required only verbal and somatic components to cast.
