## Spinner of Shadows

The Spinner of Shadows was a great demon tied to Lolth that became trapped in the world of Eberron in ages past, becoming one of the Overlords. She was eventually freed by the Lords of Dust and proceeded to leave Eberron for the Demonweb Pits, where she was absorbed into the greater whole of Lolth.The exact nature of the Spinner is left vague beyond being a powerful demon that considers Lolth her sister, and them having an uncharacteristic mutual respect for each other. She could plausibly be a very powerful demon, a lost independent avatar, an aspect, an exarch, or something stranger. As it implies that they know of each other but have not previously met, it may be that the Spinner was created at the same time as Lolth from Araushnee, truly making them sisters.

Description

The Spinner took the form of an enormous drider, resembling a muscular drow woman with the lower body of a monstrous spider, bedecked in ornate chitinous armor and carrying an immense and jagged polearm.

Personality

The Spinner was highly manipulative, arrogant, and cruel, just like Lolth. She was reputed to be the most clever and cunning of all the Overlords, and best able to control events through the skillful manipulation of people while remaining unseen and unknown. She valued nothing outside herself and Lolth (whom she considered her sister), seeing everything and everyone else as a disposable tool or an obstacle to be overcome. She even considered the Lords of Dust and the other Overlords to be a distraction from her goal of reaching Lolth. She was perfectly capable of seeming seductive and sweet when needed, though her true nature was harsh and horrid.

Like Lolth, she highly favored drow and they made up most of her cult, with females in positions of prominence. She considered all spiders great and small to be her children, and valued them well above any mortal servants.

Powers
The Spinner was grossly powerful for a demon and her mere presence was enough to paralyze or mentally dominate mere mortals. Only when she was directly constrained by the divine power of the Silver Flame's avatar was it even possible for someone to act against her. She possessed a deadly venom that was slow but lethal, and was untreatable outside of the direct intervention of divine power. Her connection to her spiders was so strong that she could feel their deaths, letting her gain awareness of potential foes within her domain.

History
The Skein of Shadows

In the ancient Age of Demons the Spinner of Shadows caused great havoc and suffering through her webs of deceit. She was eventually imprisoned in the deeply buried Spinner's Prison and kept under guard by the Silver Flame Guardian and the Church of the Silver Flame. While in this prison she was unable to influence the wider world but gathered a cult of fanatical drow who heard her whispers, drawn from the Umbragen who dwelled in the depths of Khyber in the region of Tarath Marad. There they built the City of Shadows and dominated the caverns, enslaving the duergar and living with the Spinner's many spider children, who watched over her followers and used them as a food source. The wider caverns were home to a great profusion of spiderkin, including chitines.

The Lords of Dust rakshasa Shavkar unearthed an entrance to Tarath Marad and used the promise of treasure to lure surfacers into delving into tunnels at Trent's Well. He also used the Spinner-worshiper Xujil as a guide for hire, allowing him to monitor and control the situation as needed. This culminated in him using a poorly understood prophecy to entice House Deneith into sending Tilde ir'Thul into the depths with Xujil, thinking that fulfilling the prophecy would grant them a valuable and powerful artifact. In truth, the full prophecy related to releasing the Spinner from her bonds. Tilde's expedition was destroyed in the process, and the unfortunate sorceress fell victim to the grand demonic presence of the Spinner. Following the compulsions forced upon her, she found the treasure she sought—an enormous dragonshard embedded in a demonic spider-shaped artifact that attacked her.

In response to this failed expedition, Sabira d'Deneith was sent by her House in another attempt to meet the conditions of the prophecy, once again with Xujil as a guide. Like Tilde, Sabira's group took heavy casualties before reaching the City of Shadows, and was lured into a trap while meeting Tilde again. Unfortunately for everyone, Tilde was not dead but had been consumed by the Spinner's power, turned into an insane drider-like monstrosity with a metal lower body and the dragonshard sphere embedded in her abdomen. After a torturous battle, Sabira and her sole remaining companion Greddark managed to destroy Tilde and the artifact, escaped, then narrowly survived an encounter with Shavkar. Although they had survived the trials and defeated the agents of the Spinner, Sabira understood that they had actually fulfilled the prophecy and completed a step in the Spinner's escape.

The Web of Chaos

Not long afterwards during the Darkening and the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the mid-1480s DR of Faerûn, the rakshasa Gnomon located the hidden Spinner's Prison in the depths of Khyber and put together a force to free first her and then more fiendish Overlords, hoping to usher in a new Age of Demons. The cultists like Karas and Sabriz Rinzyn Kho were aided in this task by the whispered instructions of the Spinner, who also directed them to gather up as many dragonshards as possible before freeing her.

Though Gnomon's scheme was detected by the forces of the Silver Flame, the cultists managed to enter the road to Tarath Marad and successfully weakened the wards that contained her, with flame eater spiders devouring the very essence of the Silver Flame Guardian. When the rakshasas requested her to aid them in freeing more Overlords, she simply slew them and announced her sole loyalty to her sister Lolth, calling out to the goddess. In the battle that followed the champions of the Silver Flame attempted to restore the wards and trap the Spinner again, while the Spinner used her flame eaters to weaken the Silver Flame Guardian and called on the local demons of Khyber to serve her in battle. The forces of the Silver Flame managed to relight the wards in the end, only for Lolth to personally intervene and create a Rift Between Worlds from the Demonweb Pits, allowing the Spinner to escape into the Abyss and take the dragonshards with her. The Silver Flame, the Twelve, and the Gatekeepers then arrived to secure the location, only slightly relieved that the Overlord had fled into another plane instead of conquering Eberron, but also horrified to discover that the Rift was still expanding and would eventually pull the whole world into the Demonweb as a prize for Lolth anyway.

After reaching the Demonweb, the Spinner was met by Lolth and offered the chance to merge with the Spider Goddess. The Spinner readily agreed and was consumed by Lolth, becoming part of the greater divinely demonic being. The dragonshards she had stolen were then used to create the Orbs of Lolth—tools to contain the power of Mystra and the Weave as part of Lolth's bid to become a greater goddess of magic. The heroes of the Silver Flame went on to explore the Demonweb, meet Elminster, and fight the forces of Lolth in Cormyr. After first saving Eveningstar from the Sschindylryn invasion, they eventually undid Lolth's schemes with the Thread of the Weave and definitively saved the day.

## Spinner's Prison

The Spinner's Prison was the cell and lair of the Eberronian Overlord known as the Spinner of Shadows, a grossly powerful demon from ages past. Overseen by the church of the Silver Flame, it was guarded by a small part of the Flame itself, the Silver Flame Guardian.

Location

The prison was located below the city of Stormreach within the underground domain of Khyber in Eberron, not far from Tarath Marad. It could not normally be accessed from the surface until the Lords of Dust blasted a passageway that opened the road to Tarath Marad.

Structure
The underground approach to the prison was blocked by numerous magical wards that blocked fiends and other fell creatures, but mortals were able to bypass them much more easily. Nearby, the home of Brother Aurelius was used to keep an eye on the prison and maintain the wards in person.

The prison itself was a singular large chamber ringed with braziers filled with the essence of the Silver Flame. This kept the Spinner trapped in the center in a great pillar of flame, unable to act.

Two sections of wall were incomplete and connected onto rough tunnels and caverns, some filled with magma, which allowed the native demons of that realm to enter when called.

Following the departure of the Spinner, an entire wall of the prison was destroyed and pulled into the Demonweb Pits, becoming a massive and unstable Rift Between Worlds that threatened to consume the whole of Eberron.

History

The prison was formed in ages past when the demon Overlords of Eberron were defeated. While the Silver Flame Guardian held the Spinner tight, the prison was further guarded and maintained by a chosen member of the faith who swore an oath of devotion to the cause. This person was then teleported to the prison to spend many years underground maintaining the wards and communing with the physical manifestation of their god.

Around the time of the Darkening and the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the mid-1480s DR, the Lords of Dust rakshasa Gnomon used his infiltration of the Silver Flame church to locate the Spinner's Prison, and put together a plan to break in and free the trapped Overlord. Using the local cultists in Stormreach, they put together a great ritual to blast open the road to Tarath Marad and gain access to the sealed prison. The investigators of the Silver Flame discovered the conspiracy too late and the cult managed to work their way through the wards, using the Spinner's flame eater spiders to devour and weaken the Silver Flame Guardian. The agents of the Silver Flame arrived shortly after and engaged in battle with the partially-free Spinner, with only the efforts of the Silver Flame Guardian keeping the demonic lord from simply destroying them. Even with the Spinner calling forth wild demons from Khyber the wards were relit, trapping her once more, only for Lolth herself to take notice and reach into Eberron from the Demonweb Pits. She broke open the Rift Between Worlds and beckoned the Spinner through, taking the amassed dragonshards with her and leaving Eberron behind.

The forces of the Silver Flame, the Gatekeepers, and the Twelve arrived at the location afterwards. They were relieved to find that the Spinner had not stayed to conquer Eberron, but were aghast at the enormous portal. Tavnar Kronnak understood that holding their ground would just lead to the Demonweb devouring Eberron, and so assembled a team of heroes to plunge into the Demonweb first and find a solution. These brave sorts ventured into the cursed domain of Lolth, luckily meeting Elminster and finding a path to the Underdark below Eveningstar, where they helped undo Lolth's schemes and saved Eberron in the process.

## Spindle-disk

A spindle-disk or spindle-disks were a weapon and an amusing curio used by Cadderly Bonaduce.

Description
It was composed of two razor-sharp disks as large as a human man's fist and constructed of hard rock crystal. These were connected via a central axle that acted as a spindle. This was attached to a string some  with a loop tied into the end, so as to be placed over the finger, allowing it to return to the hand when thrown. Cadderly improved on the original design by using a metal bar at the axle, with a hole through it that the string could be tied onto.

Usage
In combat, a wielder flicked the spindle-disks outward toward the opponent, making the disks spin forward to deal damage. Spindle-disks must be manually rewound if they hit their target, but if they missed they rolled back to the wielder's palm, automatically rewinding for the next attack. An attack from spindle-disks could be used to incapacitate or disarm a foe.

History
They were based on ancient halfling weapons from the tribes of southern Luiren.

By the mid–14 century DR, the weapon was mostly forgotten about apart from being described in books. From these books, Cadderly built his own spindle-diskes and improved the design, using it as a weapon in his adventures.

Notable Users
 Cadderly Bonaduce, a noted adventurer of the mid-to-late 14 century DR, a Chosen cleric of Deneir formerly of the Edificant Library in Erlkazar, and the founder of the great library-cathedral of Spirit Soaring.

Appendix
See Also
 Yo-yo of fate

Background
The spindle-disks are of course based on the yo-yo and the mistaken belief that they were used as weapons in ancient times.

## Spell amber

Spell amber was a magical substance that was formed when a living spell was destroyed.

Description
Shards of spell amber resembled mundane amber or even simple chunks of amber-colored tinted glass.

Powers
Spell amber was a valuable reagent that only the most skilled eyes could distinguish from mundane glass or amber. These shards could be used to restore enchanted lifeforged items to full power.

History
In the late 15 century DR, spell amber fragments could be found scattered across the River District of Neverwinter, during the city's adventurers' and defenders struggle against the rising threat of the Cloaked Ascendancy.

## Spool

Spool was a commoner child who lived in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Spool was a young human boy of bronze skin. His eyes were chestnut in color, and his light brown hair was trimmed short.

History
In the, Spool could be found in the Lower City with his two friends, Stinker and Runt, often playing "chase the gelatinous cube" in the alleys or loitering in front of Devil's Fee shop, daring eachother to go into the "devil's house" and accusing eachother of being scaredy-cats. Notably, Spool was unenthused by Devil's Fee, thinking the kids' daring of each other to be boring.
